7～9 月期実質GDP は年率＋1.0％と 
プラス成長を予想（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比＋0.2％（年率＋1.0％）と、4～6月期（前期比＋
0.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半期連続でのプラス成長を予想 
○ 個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資や民間在庫が押し上げる見込み。なお、公的在庫と輸入では
中東情勢を主因とする一時的な変動が生じ、成長率にプラス・マイナス両面で影響 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）はプラス成長 を
予想 
11月16日 （月） に、 内閣府から2026年7～9月期の 「四半期別GDP速 報（QE）」
（1次速報値）が公表される。現時点で入手可能な基礎統計の結果を踏まえ
ると、7～9月期の実質GDPは前期比＋0.2％（ 年 率＋1.0％） となる見込みだ。
4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半
期連続でプラス成長になると予想する（図表）。 
7～9月期は、内需の柱である個人消費が伸び悩む一方、 設備投資の拡大や、
製造業を中心とする在庫積み増しが成長率を押し上げるとみられる。その他
では、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要因として現れる
が、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイナス要因になるなど、 
図表 2026 年7～9 月期GDP（1 次速報）予測

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

### Page 2

2

中東情勢による一時的な変動も引き続き成長率に影響する見込みだ。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
個人消費は伸び悩み 。耐
久財の特需剥落や、 根強
い節約志向が影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％（4～6月期：同＋0.0％） と、2四半期連続で伸び
悩む結果になろう。消費の弱さの背景には、 複合的な要因があると考えられ
る。 
耐久財では、自動車とエアコンの「特需」剥落が押し下げ要因になろう。
自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に廃止されたこと
を受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていたが、8月以降は再
び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年4月の省エネ基準
引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要が生じていたが、
景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入った後は一服した
模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減少もエアコンなど
季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
食品やエネルギー、 各種消耗品などの非耐久財では、物価高を背景とする
根強い節約志向により、 弱含みが続いているようだ。今回のインフレ局面が
本格化する前の2020年と比較すると、非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6
年半でおよそ1.3倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上
昇が、 消費者の体感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、 購入数量の抑制
につながっている可能性がある。 
サービス消費は、 イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、 サービスや価格帯、 顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。 また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・ 宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、 レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投 資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
設備投資は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.9％） と、3四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスへ転じると予想する。 
直近の設備投資関連指標をみると、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財
総供給指数（除く輸送機械）が7～8月に大きく上昇した。また第3次産業活
動指数では、ソフトウェア業の実質売上高が同じく7～8月に増加したことが
確認でき、どちらも7～9月期の設備投資が拡大したことを示唆している。 
加えて、前期の4～6月期に発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特許権の海外
譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少） がなくなったこともプラス要因だ。た
だし、 これは裏を返せば、7～9月期のQEにおける設備投資の伸びが実態以上
に強い数字になるということでもあり、評価する際には留意が必要である。 
住宅投資は 前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.8％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.6％ ）と、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスとなろう。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～8月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上

### Page 3

3

昇による需要低迷に加えて、 足元では地震や台風などによる着工 ・ 進捗の遅
れが発生している可能性がある。 
民間 在庫は 2四半期連続
で成長率にプラス寄与 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、＋0.2％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラスが続く見込みだ。AI関連需要の強さなどを背景に、製
造業で在庫積み増しの動きが進んでいることが影響すると考えられる。な
お、4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を
押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足
が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響は一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要 は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラスに 
公的需要は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.8％）と、2四半期ぶりの前期
比プラスを予想する。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じるとみられることだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変
動の寄与度が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備
蓄の減少が小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公
的在庫の減少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.3％Pt程度のプラ
ス寄与になると見込まれる。 
その他の公的需要項目では、政府消費を前期比＋0.2％（4～6月期：同＋
1.7％）と予想する。4～6月期は高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化開始に
より高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした特殊要因が剥落し、緩やかな
増加ペースに戻ると考えられる。公共投資は前期比▲0.5％（4～6月期：同
▲0.5％）と、土木関連工事の減少を受けて2四半期連続のマイナスを見込む。 
輸出が小幅に減少する 一
方、 輸入が増加し、外需は
4四半期ぶりのマイナス
寄与 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.3％（4～6月期：同＋0.4％）と小幅に減少す
る一方、輸 入が同＋1.3％（4～6月 期：同▲1.7％）と増加に転じる見込みだ。
実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.3％Ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナ
スが予想される。 
輸入の増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化により4
～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホルム
ズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの輸入
量が確保されている。 
輸入はGDPの控除項目であることから、7～9月期は輸入増加が成長率の下
押し要因になる。 ただし、代替調達の進展に伴って国家備蓄原油の放出が抑
制され、 先述したように7～9月期は公的在庫変動が成長率にプラス寄与する
とみられることから、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果の一部は公的在庫変
動により相殺されるだろう。 
輸出では、前期の4～6月期に特許権の海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げ
た （これは4～6月期の設備投資の押し下げ要因でもある） 反動が現れ、 一時
的に下振れするだろう。一方、旺盛なAI関連需要が支えになり、半導体・電

### Page 4

4

子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸出は堅調に推移する見込みだ。インバウ
ンド需要 （非居住者家計の国内での直接購入） は、 日中対立の継続による中
国人訪日客の少なさに加え、 航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の伸び悩みに
より、低調な結果になると見込まれる。 
2026 年度後半 の実質 GDP
は小幅プラス と予想 。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期から減速するものの、4四半期
連続でプラス成長になると予想する。GDPの約半分を占める個人消費の伸び
悩みが足を引っ張っているが、設備投資や製造業を中心とする在庫積み増し
がけん引する格好だ。 
先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～2027年3月）の 実質GDP
は、 均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。個人消費が振るわな
い一方で、 設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろう。2026年度全体の
実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平均水準が押し上げら
れることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％程度とまずまずの
成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。 原油 ・ ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、 コア消費者物価指数 （生鮮食品を除く総合） は年度末にかけて前年比＋
3％程度まで上昇すると予想される（ただし、2026年末から政府の電気・ガ
ス料金支援策が実施されれば、0.4～0.5％Pt程度の押し下げ要因になる可能
性がある）。加えて、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを
中心とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、 一時的に消費が落ち込む可能
性がある。 
他方で、年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調
査） では、2026年度の設備投資計画 （ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投
資額 （除く土地投資額）） が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した （6月 調 査：
同＋8.8％）。生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、
設備の不足感が強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、 設備投資は今後拡大
傾向で推移していく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」 （特定生産性向上
設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、 日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、 企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、 また、 投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、 中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。

### Page 5

5

日銀は10月会合で 政策金
利を据え置き 。次回利上
げは12月と予想 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、 日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％） は、次回12
月17・18日の会合と予想する。 前回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて 「3カ月
に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、 日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の 「半年強
に1回」から加速していることを印象付けるものになろう。 
企業の価格転嫁行動は一段と積極化しており、植田総裁が9月会合後の記
者会見で 「政策の局面が変化した」と説明したように、日本銀行は「物価引
き上げ」 から 「物価上振れ抑制」 へと金融政策運営の舵を切っている。12月
の追加利上げ後も、 金利上昇の影響を確認しながら、 日銀は最終的に2027年
半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げていくだろう。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

## Document 083: 2308台達電 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/2308台達電 群益.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 29 日

1 群益投顧 
台達電 (2308 TT) 公布 3Q26 營收為 1,970.59 億元、 QoQ+7.5% ； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影 響，毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ，營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6% ；稅 後 淨 利 為 234.95 億 元、QoQ -6.5% ，
稅後 EPS 為 9.04 元 。 隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、 Vera Rubin 產品 量產出貨 ， 預期 4Q26 營運
將有顯著提升 。 受 惠 於 AI 、 Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長 ， 營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 ，
投資評等為 Buy 。

研 究 員 ： 王 美 珍   
iris.wang@capital.com.tw

3Q26 稅後 EPS 為 9.0 4 元 ：  
台 達 電 公 布 3Q26 營 收 為 1, 970.59 億 元 、 QoQ+ 7.5%； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影響 ， 毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ， 營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6 %； 稅 後 淨 利 為 234.9 5 億 元 、
QoQ -6.5 %， 稅 後 EPS 為 9. 04 元 ， 低 於 預 期 。  
產 品 規 格 提 升 ， 電源 類 產 品 展 望 佳：  
台達電 為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案 主 要 供 應 商， 受 惠 於 AI 需 求 提 升 ， 帶 動
AC/DC 電 源 大 幅 成 長 外 ，CSP 客 戶 在 新 一 代 ASIC 伺 服 器 平 台 採 用 DC/DC
電 源 模 組 ， 亦 成 為 另 一 成 長 動 能 。800 VDC 與 ± 400VDC 預 計 於 4Q26 出貨 ，
2026 年 營 收 比 重 約 1% ，預 期 2H27 營 收 貢 獻 較 顯 著。  
水 冷 散 熱 2026 年 營 收 維 持 成 長 趨 勢：  
水冷散熱產品有水冷板、 Manifold 、 CDU 、 Sidecar 。目前出貨以系統類的
Sidecar 為 主 ， 歸 類 於 基 礎 建 設； CDU 則 歸 類 於 電 源 類 與 零 組 件 。 水 冷 散 熱
2025 年 營 收 比 重 為 9%，台 達 電 Sidecar 產品 2025 年 市 佔 率 極 高，雖 有 競 爭
者 加 入 ， 但 整 體 市 場 需 求 持 續 增 長 ，2026 年 Sidecar 營 收 仍 將 維 持 成 長 趨
勢，預估 2026 年 水 冷 散 熱 營 收 比 重 為 12% 。  
固 態 變 壓 器 為 長 期 成 長 動 能：  
SST (固 態 變 壓器 )目 前 已 少 量 出 貨，客 戶 為 中 系 CSP 、800V 高 壓 直 流 電 動 車
充 電 站，亦 出 貨 給 美 系 客 戶 測 試。SST 導 入 需 從 資 料 中 心 設 計 初 期 即 納 入 規
劃，預期  2~3 年 後 會 產 生 顯 著 的 營 收 貢 獻 ， 仍 持 續 改 良 產 品 規 格 中 。 
投 資 建 議 ：  
隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、Vera Rubin 產 品 量 產 出 貨，預 期 4Q26 營 運 將 有 顯 著 提 升。
受惠於 AI 、 Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長 ， 營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 ， 投 資 評
等為 Buy 。

產業速報

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아 쉬 웠 습 니 다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추 정 치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324.pdf

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

HD현대중공업 (329180)

HD현대중공업 투자의견 및 목표주가 변동추이

## Document 267: AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/6 
AI 関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか 
～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 世界的な AI ブームが続くなか、AI 関連企業がデータセンター建設などの巨額投資を賄うた
めに社債発行や借入による資金調達を急増させている。「AI関連企業は手元資金が潤沢で財
務が盤石だ」という従来の常識は揺らぎつつあり、企業の資金不足や信用リスクに目を向け
る必要がある。 
○ AI 投資の拡大は3 つの変化を通じて、企業の財務と金融市場にリスクをもたらしうる。第一
に、テクノロジー企業の社債発行が急増し、借入依存が強まっている。第二に、巨額の設備
投資がAI大手の現金創出力（フリーキャッシュフロー）を圧迫している。第三に、信用力の
低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒が強まり、信用スプレッドが拡大している。 
○ 「AIの成長期待さえあれば巨額の投資もすべて回収できる」と安易に想定すべきではない。
AI 投資の資金を借入などの外部資金に頼る度合いが高まる環境では、収益化の遅れが企業の
信用力低下に直結しやすく、従来の資産配分についても再点検が必要となる。 
○ 個人投資家にとっては、保有する社債ファンドの業種偏りや信用リスクを点検することと、
株式運用において特定の AI 銘柄への過度な集中を避け、安定したキャッシュフローを生む
資産を補完的に組み入れることが、対応策の一つとなるだろう。

1. 「キャッシュリッチなAI企業」の変調 
生成AIの普及を原動力とする世界的なハイテク株高は、日本の個人投資家にとっても資産形成
の大きな追い風となってきた。NISA（少額投資非課税制度）などを通じて米国株式（S&P500な
ど）や全世界株式を保有する投資家の多くは、AI関連企業の成長や株価上昇の恩恵を受けてきた
といえる。もっとも、足元ではAI関連株の値動きが荒くなる場面も増え、巨額の投資が本当に利
益につながるのか不安を感じている投資家も少なくないだろう。一方で、市場には依然として「A
I企業の財務は盤石であり、足元の揺らぎは一時的な調整にすぎない」とみる楽観論も根強い。し
かし、企業の「台所事情」に目を向けると、これまでとは異なるリスクが見えてくる。 「AIブー
ム」と聞くと、華やかな技術革新や株価の上昇ばかりに目が向きがちだが、企業財務の視点に立
つと、巨額のデータセンター投資が企業の手元資金を圧迫し、一部のAI関連企業では借入などの

### Page 2

2/6 
外部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
華やかなAI株高の裏側で膨らむ巨額の借金を、AI関連企業は本当に返せるのだろうか。 「テクノ
ロジー企業は潤沢な現金を持ち、財務が健全だ」という従来の常識にとらわれていると、資金不
足や信用リスクの拡大という構造的な変化を見落としかねない。本稿では、AI投資の拡大が引き
起こす 3つの変化として、 「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用スプレッドの拡大」を整
理し、個人投資家が資産運用において注意すべき論点を提示したい。なお、本稿では、AI投資を
主導する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）から、AI関連事業を手掛ける中堅企業ま
でを幅広く「AI関連企業」と総称する。また、 「テクノロジー企業」は、社債発行統計などにおい
てテクノロジー（IT）部門に分類される企業を指す用語として用いる。

2. AI投資の拡大はなぜ資金不足と信用リスクを招くのか 
(1) AI投資の拡大で急増するテクノロジー企業の社債発行 
現在のAIブームを牽引する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）をはじめとするAI関
連企業は、データセンターの建設や先端半導体の確保、電力インフラの整備などに巨額の資金を
投じている。これまでテクノロジー企業は、株式発行や潤沢な手元資金を主な資金源としてきた
が、投資規模の急拡大に伴い、資金調達のあり方が大きく変わりつつある。 
RBA（豪州準備銀行）が2026 年10月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」によれば、AI
関連企業は大規模な投資計画を賄うため、社債やプライベート・クレジット、銀行借入など、負
債による資金調達への依存を強めている。資料1の上段は米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占め
るテクノロジー部門の割合を、下段はハイパースケーラー（巨大クラウド・AI企業）とテクノロ
ジー部門の実際の社債発行額を示したものである。2026年には、米国の投資適格社債の発行総額
に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が約27％に達している。また、下段が示すように、足元ではハ
イパースケーラーによる起債が急増し、部門全体の社債発行額を大きく押し上げている。RBAは、
今後計画されているAI関連の設備投資のうち3分の1以上が負債性資金によって賄われるとの市
場の見方を紹介している。つまり、AIブームが続くほど、テクノロジー企業の借入依存が強ま
り、公募・私募の債券市場全体がAI投資の成否に左右されやすくなるという新たな構図が生まれ
ている。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 テクノロジー部門の社債発行が急増、米国投資適格社債の発行総額に占める割合は約 27％に 
（注）米国の非金融企業による投資適格社債の発行額（期間は 2010 年 3 月～2026 年 7 月 17 日）。テクノロジー部門にはコンピ
ュータ・電子機器を含む。上段の割合は 4 四半期移動平均。ハイパースケーラーは Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle、NVIDIA を指す。 
（出所）Reserve Bank of Australia, “Financial Stability Review – October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 巨額の設備投資が企業の現金創出力を圧迫 
なぜ、高い収益力を誇る巨大テクノロジー企業までもが借入への依存を強めているのか。その
背景には、データセンター構築などの設備投資があまりにも巨額であり、本業の稼ぐ力（営業利
益）だけでは投資資金を賄いきれなくなっている現実がある。 
IMF（国際通貨基金）が2026 年10月に公表した「Global Financial Stability Report」は、
AI投資の中心を担う主要ハイパースケーラー5社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle）の財務状況を分析している。資料2は、これら 5社の設備投資（Capex：Capital 
Expenditure） 、営業利益、およびフリーキャッシュフローの推移を示したものである。5社の設備
投資は 2023年頃から急拡大し、2025年後半から2026年にかけて営業利益を上回る規模に達して
いる。その結果、フリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）は急減し、2023～24年のピーク時には四半期
ベースで600 億米ドルを超えていたものが、2026年にはほぼゼロまで落ち込んでいる。もっと
も、ハイパースケーラー各社は現時点では強固なバランスシートと十分な流動性を維持してい
る。しかし、激しい競争圧力や技術の陳腐化に対応するため、企業が投資の手を緩めることは難
しい。今後、AIの収益化が市場の期待を下回るようなことがあれば、企業の現金創出力は一段と
圧迫され、追加の資金調達や財務負担の増大を招く可能性がある。

### Page 4

4/6

資料 2 主要ハイパースケーラー5 社の設備投資が急増し、2026 年のフリーキャッシュフローはほぼゼロに 
（注）主要ハイパースケーラー5 社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracle）の設備投資（Capex、左軸）、営業利益（左軸）、
およびフリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）の四半期ごとの推移（単位：10 億米ドル）。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒 
さらに、AI投資への借入依存が高まるなか、社債市場ではテクノロジー企業の信用リスクに対
する警戒が強まりつつある。株式市場ではAIの成長期待が注目されやすいが、元利金の確実な返
済を重視する債券市場では、企業の財務体力やビジネスモデルの持続可能性に対する選別がシビ
アに進んでいる。 
ECB（欧州中央銀行）が2026 年5月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」では、AIを巡
る懸念が株式市場だけでなく社債市場にも波及していると指摘している。資料3 は、2025年 3月
から 2026年5 月にかけてのユーロ圏および米国におけるハイイールド（HY、低格付け）社債の信
用スプレッドの推移を示したものである。米国・ユーロ圏ともに、ハイイールド債市場全体（米
国HY、ユーロ圏HY）のスプレッドが300ベーシスポイント（bp）前後で比較的落ち着いて推移し
ていたのに対し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業（米国HYテック、ユーロ圏HYテック）のスプレ
ッドは 2026年に入り500bp超へと大きく拡大し、同年 5月時点でも450bp前後と、市場全体を上
回る水準で推移していた。 
ECBは、AIが既存のソフトウェア企業などのビジネスモデルを脅かすリスクに加え、内部資金だ
けでなく借入に頼ってAI投資を拡大させる動きが金融安定上の懸念になりうると指摘している。
巨額のAI投資ブームの裏側では、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する市場の警戒が強まり、
企業の借入依存が信用市場にも新たなリスクをもたらしつつある。 「借りた巨額の資金を本当に返

### Page 5

5/6 
せるのか」という市場の問いかけがすでに始まっているのである。

資料 3 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業の信用スプレッドはハイイールド債市場全体より大きく拡大 
（注）2025 年 3 月 1 日～2026 年 5 月 19 日におけるユーロ圏および米国のハイイールド（HY）社債の信用スプレッド（OIS カーブ 
に対する上乗せ金利、時価総額加重平均、単位：ベーシスポイント）。「HY テック」はハイイールド格付けのテクノロジー企業
を指す。 
縦の破線は左から 2025 年 4 月の米国の関税発表、前回 FSR（金融安定報告書）公表時点（2025 年 11 月）、中東戦争の開 
始（2026 年 3 月初旬）を示す。 
（出所）European Central Bank, “Financial Stability Review, May 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 「AI借金時代」の資産防衛術 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI ブームの裏側では「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用
スプレッドの拡大」という3 つの変化が同時に進行している。この視点に立てば、AIブームが抱
える死角は「株価の割高感」だけでは説明できない。巨大テクノロジー企業から中堅のテクノロ
ジー企業に至るまで、AI投資の拡大に伴い、一部の企業では自己資金だけでなく、借入などの外
部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
ここで重要なのは、 「テクノロジー企業はキャッシュリッチで財務が盤石だ」と安易に考えない
ことである。仮にAI の収益化が遅れれば、膨らんだ借入負担が企業の業績や信用力を直撃するシ
ナリオも視野に入れる必要がある。こうした「AI借金時代」を迎えるなか、個人投資家には、従
来のポートフォリオに潜む信用リスクを改めて点検することが求められる。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
第一に、社債ファンドやハイイールド債（低格付け社債）における「信用リスク」を過小評価
しないことである。2026年の米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が
約27％に達し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業のスプレッドが拡大している現状では、 「債券だか
ら安心」 「株安を社債がカバーしてくれる」という従来の前提が働きにくい可能性がある。AI の収
益期待が後退する局面では、AI 関連株の下落と信用スプレッドの拡大（社債価格の下落）が同時
に進みかねない点に注意が必要だ。債券を保有する場合には、表面的な利回りの高さだけでハイ
イールド債や社債ファンドを選好するのではなく、組入資産の業種の偏りを確認することや、信
用リスクの低い先進国国債などへの分散を検討することも、選択肢の一つとなる。 
第二に、株式運用において「AI関連だから買う」という一括りの発想から脱却し、企業の現金
創出力と業種分散を重視することである。NISAなどを活用してS&P500や全世界株式連動商品を保
有する投資家は多いが、これらの指数では、米国の巨大テクノロジー企業の構成比率が高まって
おり、特にS&P500では一部の企業への集中が進んでいる。指数連動商品をポートフォリオの中核
に据えつつも、補完的な資産として、安定したフリーキャッシュフローを生み出す高配当バリュ
ー株やディフェンシブ株、あるいはAIデータセンター投資の受け皿となる電力・インフラ関連株
などを組み入れることは、AI 企業の資金不足や淘汰の波に備える選択肢の一つとなるだろう。た
だし、特定のテーマや業種に偏りすぎないよう、投資比率には十分な注意が必要である。 
AI 革命は株式市場に大きな成長機会をもたらす一方で、企業の財務構造や信用市場のリスクを
も変えつつある。これからは、 「AIだから成長する」という発想にとどまらず、その裏側で膨らむ
「借金の行方」まで見据えることが重要になる。AI 企業のフリーキャッシュフローや信用スプレ
ッドの動きも、株価と同様に注視する。それが、巨額のAI投資時代において投資家に求められる
新たな視点であろう。

以 上

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

### Page 4

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1810 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 303: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Pages: 9

### Page 1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a 
headwind across all income cohorts

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL

(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 411: The nowcasts, updated scenario and forecasts of the Economic Research - 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The nowcasts, updated scenario and forecasts of the Economic Research -  2 November 2026 (2026-11-02).pdf

economic

In the meantime, you can read our analysis of the third
-
quarter growth figures 
here

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2
nd 
Novem
ber 2026

### Page 2

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.6% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of German defence spending on the real economy . Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we anticipate another 
25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That said, strong er than 
expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q  in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aero nautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before  rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
US-China talks, a possible Trump-Xi-Putin trilateral meeting, and any signs of a China-Japan diplomatic
thaw will be critical watchpoints. The priorities of the summit between representatives of 21 Asia-Pacific
economies are set to include efforts to boost innovation, digital connectivity, supply-chain resilience and

support for an open multilateral trading system. During the summit, presidents Trump and Xi are expected to
meet for the third time this year. This meeting will provide signals on the state of China-US talks, including the
operationalisation of new bilateral trade and investment boards, and on the likelihood of another extension of
the Busan trade truce beyond January 2027. President Trump has also indicated he is open to a trilateral US-
China-Russia meeting during the summit, possibly covering nuclear arms control and the Russia-Ukraine war.

Investors will also closely watch whether Japanese Prime Minister Takaichi and President Xi meet on the
sidelines, following reports that Tokyo is hoping to arrange a meeting. However, the bar for a formal
breakthrough will likely remain high. As we noted in a report earlier this year, a full normalisation of relations
and an easing of Chinese economic restrictions – which include rare-earth export controls – may require a
lengthy period of diplomatic re-engagement and backchannel diplomacy.

A divided government could mean less change at home but more abroad. Third-party polling and betting
markets forecast Democrat control of the House, and an increasing probability of flipping the Senate. 80% of
respondents in our monthly poll now say living costs will influence their vote – the most since April. For
markets, a divided government could mean less domestic legislative change, but not necessarily a more
predictable US foreign policy. Losing Congress could leave theadministration’s geopolitical and geoeconomic

toolkit largely intact.
In our view, weapons stockpiles, inflationary pressures and long-end yields may matter more than Congress
in constraining theadministration’s choices in 2027 and 2028. A Democratic House could dial up oversight of
the administration, launch investigations, force votes on War Powers Resolutions, and use appropriations bills
to press for restrictions on departmental funding. This may generate a steady stream of attention-grabbing

headlines, but whether it decisively affects the event-driven risk that matters for markets (e.g. blocking a tariff
or preventing a limited military strike) remains in doubt. If domestic policymaking becomes more challenging,
foreign policy could become a top priority for the Trump administration.

US midterm elections (3 November)
APEC Economic Leaders’ Meeting in Shenzhen (18-19 November)

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
NewZealand’s general election is shaping up as a close and fragmented contest. Both the ruling National
and opposition Labour parties are polling at historically low levels, and smaller parties will likely determine
the next government. In a closely contested vote, investors could see coalition uncertainty and a wider

range of possible outcomes for tax, spending, infrastructure, energy and migration policy.
The National party is campaigning on a platform that would broadly preserve the current emphasis on
fiscal restraint, lower regulation and private-sector investment. A Labour-led government could place
greater weight on public services, housing and climate policy, alongside a proposed capital-gains tax on
investment property. However, because any government will rely on minor parties for survival, the final

policy mix will depend heavily on post-election negotiations.
The meeting will be a test of the EU’s ability to translate the competitiveness agenda into practical
reforms. The focus will be on Europe’s ability to restore competitiveness, mobilize investment and
maintain fiscal discipline. The Eurogroup brings together Euro-area finance ministers and is expected to
consider macroeconomic and fiscal developments, energy market pressures and progress towards the

Savings and Investments Union. ECOFIN brings together all EU finance ministers, and will focus on
financial integration, banking sector competitiveness, and economic coordination. To watch will be
tensions between fiscal consolidation and the need to fund defence, energy security and strategic
investment, alongside progress on integratingEurope’s fragmented capital markets and banking sectors. A
budget-focused Council meeting follows on 10 November.

Implementation of existing commitments, rather than new policy pledges, is expected to be the primary
focus of the next climate summit in Antalya. Building on the shift towards implementation at COP30,
COP31 will focus on translating existing climate commitments into practical action, including through
investment, financing and partnerships. For investors, the key watchpoint will be whether the summit can
deliver tangible commitments that translate existing plans into binding funding pledges for critical

infrastructure resilience, scaled-up renewable energy output, and climate finance.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics: November 2026: Key events, themes and trends to watch in the month ahead |  November 2026
Melaka’s state election will offer a further gauge of Malaysia’s evolving electoral landscape after
strong recent state-level performances by Barisan Nasional (BN). The contest will also be watched for
its read-through to Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s Pakatan Harapan (PH), following setbacks in other

state elections this year.
BN enters the election from a position of relative strength, while its electoral understanding with PAS
and its allies could provide an early indication of how political alignments are evolving ahead of the next
general election. However, Melaka’s local dynamics mean the result should not be treated as a direct
national forecast.
The anticipated signing of a landmark digital economy agreement is set to be one of the most

significant summit deliverables in years. The 49th ASEAN Summit in Manila will focus on economic
integration, energy security, supply-chain resilience, maritime security, the crisis in Myanmar, and
relations with external partners.
For markets, the formal signing of the ASEAN Digital Economy Framework Agreement (DEFA) is the key
event to watch. Designed as a legally binding treaty, DEFA aims to harmonize regional digital trade rules
and cross-border data flows. Successful implementation is expected to double the projected size of the

region’s digital economy by 2030, boosting it from a projected baseline of US$1 trillion to US$2 trillion
(up from approximately US$300 billion today).
The second EU-UK Summit will be an important test of whether the post-Brexit relationship can move
towards tangible outcomes. Prime Minister Andy Burnham will seek to finalize agreements on food and
agricultural standards, youth mobility, and linking emissions trading systems. Burnham has framed
relations as “not good enough.” To watch will be whether we see concrete deals versus declarations, and

whether leaders are prepared to make the concessions necessary to deepen ties.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in November 2026
Listed in chronological order
49th ASEAN Summit (16-18 November)
Source: istockphoto
